## Task B+E: Clean bjb1 /tmp/ray + run c_prime_step5_ood backup on bjb1 cuda:0

Server: bjb1-17606 (root account)

### Task E: Clean /tmp/ray first
```bash
rm -rf /tmp/ray/session_latest_* 2>/dev/null
ls /tmp/ray/ | head -20
df -h /tmp
df -h /
df -h /root/autodl-tmp/
```
Report disk space after cleanup.

### Task B: Run c_prime_step5_ood on bjb1 cuda:0

The C' step 5 HF checkpoint should already be on bjb1 from the FSDP→HF conversion.

1. Find the checkpoint:
```bash
ls -la /root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/ | grep c_prime
# Also check:
ls -la /root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/c_prime_step5_hf/ 2>/dev/null
```

2. Check/create OOD eval config. Look for existing OOD eval configs as template:
```bash
ls /root/cost-verify-agent-rl/RAGEN/config/ | grep ood
cat /root/cost-verify-agent-rl/RAGEN/config/_alfworld_c_prime_step5_ood_eval.yaml 2>/dev/null
```
If no config exists, create one based on an existing OOD eval config (e.g., the intervention_c or seed7 OOD configs). Key settings:
- val_only mode
- 134 OOD episodes (valid_unseen split)
- checkpoint path pointing to the c_prime_step5_hf location on bjb1
- cuda:0 only
- The checkpoint needs a "qwen" in its path for get_special_tokens() to work (symlink if needed: `ln -sf actual_path /root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/qwen_c_prime_step5_hf`)

3. Submit using `submit_training_job`:
   - Environment: `source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base`
   - Working dir: `/root/cost-verify-agent-rl/RAGEN/`
   - GPU: cuda:0

4. Verify GPU utilization:
```bash
nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,utilization.gpu,memory.used --format=csv,noheader
```

**IMPORTANT**:
- This is a BACKUP eval in case westd fails. Both locations are racing — first to succeed wins.
- Register as experiment `c_prime_step5_ood_bjb1` (separate from the westd version)
- Report: new PID, disk space status, GPU status

- `resume_from_path` loads the FSDP checkpoint weights

For an HF checkpoint, I can just set `model_path` to the HF checkpoint directly, and use `resume_mode: "disable"` (no FSDP checkpoint to resume from). But the task says the checkpoint needs "qwen" in its path for `get_special_tokens()` to work, so I'll need to create a symlink. I should also match the C' intervention config settings like `max_turn: 25` and `max_response_length: 12` for consistency.

For the OOD eval setup, I'm debating whether to enable thinking since C' was trained with it, but the config doesn't explicitly set `enable_think`, so I need to figure out what the default behavior was during C' training versus what seed2_ood_step15 uses.

Rather than overthinking this, I'll adapt the seed2_ood_step15 template for C's HF checkpoint evaluation, adjusting the model path to point to the qwen_c_prime_step5_hf symlink, setting resume mode to disabled since it's an HF checkpoint rather than FSDP, enabling validation-only mode with a single GPU, and configuring it for 134 OOD episodes.

For the hyperparameters, I'm keeping C's training settings of max_response_length 12000 and max_turn 25, though I'm reconsidering whether to bump max_turn to 50 like other OOD evals do to give the model more flexibility during evaluation. I should check whether max_response_length matters for inference or if it was just a training constraint. I'm debating whether to enable thinking for the C' model eval—the seed2 OOD config disables it for consistency, but C' was trained with thinking enabled. Since I'm not sure which approach is right, I'll just create the config without explicitly setting it and let it use the default behavior.

Now I'll base the new config on the seed2 OOD template but point it to the C' checkpoint instead. Before I proceed, I should check if cuda:0 is already in use on bjb1.
